# Tasks (the live list)

**Workspace ID:** SPARMVET_VIZ  
**Last sorted:** 2026-11-13. Only open and current tasks are here. Done tasks are in `archives/`; superseded tasks, session notes and the old completed-phases table are in `outdated/tasks_before_sort_2026-11-13.md`; the original text of the backlog tasks that were shortened or merged on 2026-11-13 is in `outdated/tasks_backlog_details_2026-11-13.md`. The map of the whole `.claude` folder is `.claude/INDEX.md`.

## How to read this file

| Group | What it is | Waits for |
|---|---|---|
| 1. HOME audit build | the redesign of HOME (Exploration and Audit); packages P0-P12 of the stage-2 plan | Eve's reading of `spaces/HOME.md` and the stage-2 spec, then P0 |
| 2. Recipe format | the recipe format, checker and migration (the recipe session, issue #73) | the recipe session; the HOME build depends on part of it |
| 3. Configuration stage | names, gating, deployment resources | last, after the build |
| 4. Backlog | older tasks, not triaged with Eve | Eve's triage |

## Rules for this file

- A task is one line (or one short block) with its id, `[model/effort]` tag and, when it has one, its issue number; context goes in the design files, not here.
- Done: move the task to a file in `archives/` and add a pointer at the bottom. Superseded: move it to `outdated/` with the reason. No `Completed` notes inside this file.
- Session notes and decisions go in the design files, the guide's decision register or the ADR log, not here.
- New tasks carry a model and effort tag (`CLAUDE.md` section 2).
- Eve 2026-11-15: builds on a recommendation are PROVISIONAL (recorded with the commit, reversible) when no result changes silently and nothing is hidden; open points go to her in ONE ordered list.

---

### P1 - filters correct (issues #62, #63)

- [x] **NA-RULE-63** `[sonnet/high]` — Eve gave the rule 2026-11-12 (`home_audit_steps.md` section 19, "Meaning of the filters"): a missing value is a value; `!=` and none-of keep it, `==`, one-of and comparisons remove it, always counted and reported. Prerequisite of audit mode (the translated actions must do what the dialog says). `filter_ne` and `not_in` drop missing values today (verified); no shipped recipe uses them. Issue #63 WP3b was blocked on this rule. **RULE CHANGED, Eve DECIDED 2026-11-14 (review session):** the engine follows Polars (checked by running, Polars 1.40.1): `!=` and not-in also REMOVE rows with a missing value; the earlier "keep" meaning is withdrawn as an engine rule. Every filter step states `na: keep|remove` (required, no default); the HOME dialog may preselect by operator with a visible tick and the count; Blueprint and the checker show the counts and warn for `!=` / not-in on a column with missing values. NaN is a number (Polars): its own count, never folded into missing, treated by explicit steps (`polars_fill_nan`, `polars_drop_nans`); `na` does not touch it. Help text and user doc must explain missing, NaN and the text NA, and the Polars equivalents. Unblocked.
  Replaces HOME-EXPLORE-3a and HOME-EXPLORE-3b (issue #63): the rule is decided, so the "inform only" step is part of this one.
  - Part 1 done 2026-11-14 (merge `4b41b719`): tests of the decided rule in the engine step, the HOME table and the plot; `libs/utils/src/utils/missing_values.py` (`count_missing`, `missing_sentences`); help text and user documentation. Part 2 `[sonnet/high]`: the tick in the HOME filter dialog with the counts, HOME table and plot read `na`, the export translator writes the chosen `na` (Eve 2026-11-14: the frozen files may be edited for this; the tick and the translator ship together; the tick replaces 'NA (missing)' as a selectable value). Eve 2026-11-14: Q-NA3 to Q-NA6 need no question (HOME always makes the user choose; every audit step records it; old steps are carried as remove; offered for every operator; counted on the rows the filter acts on).
  - Part 2 built 2026-11-14 on branch `task/na-rule-b` (the tick with the counts in the HOME dialog; table, plot and results bundle read `na`; every audit step records it; the export writes it; old data carried as `remove`); in review; not merged yet. Fixes after review done 2026-11-15 (tests for four unnoticed mutations; `between` with `closed` and the chosen `na` in the export; `closed` kept on Send to Audit; the step list says kept or removed); second review passed; merged 2026-11-15, `4674b604`. The whole rule is built: the tick with counts in the HOME dialog, table, plot, results bundle and export read and write `na`, `between` exported with `closed`, old steps carried as remove.

### Actions must act (run-time guard; after the alias removal of the recipe migration, #78)

- [ ] **ACTION-MUST-ACT-1** `[opus/high]` (Eve 2026-11-13: "we need to ensure that the action does something"): a step that cannot act fails, it never returns the frame unchanged. Measured 2026-11-13: 17 of 67 actions with required parameters accept a step without them (13 return the frame unchanged, 4 change it: `keep_columns`, `summarize`, `unpivot`, `count_by_group`) and 54 ignore a key they do not declare (`filter_gt` with `column` returns every row). Do: `register_action` checks `required` and unknown keys against the `ui_schema` with the function shared with the checker (`utils.ui_schema`, recipe session), and the silent early returns of the actions go. Tests first: `libs/transformer/tests/test_actions_must_act.py` (two `xfail(strict=True)`). **Paths (verified 2026-11-13 by running the real orchestrator, `libs/transformer/tests/test_actions_must_act.py`):** a schema's wrangling goes through `DataWrangler`, which resolves the aliases `column`, `source`, `source_column`, `target_column` into `columns` (`data_wrangler.py:132-137`) and injects `columns: []` when no key is found; a join's steps go through `DataAssembler`, which resolves no alias, so there `filter_gt` with `column` changes nothing; a typo key changes nothing in both. The guard therefore belongs in `register_action` (both paths), and the alias resolution in the wrangler goes with the aliases. Order: after the aliases (`source`, `column`, `target_column`) are gone from the shipped recipes (RECIPE-MIGRATE-1, #78); `VirulenceFinder_wrangling.yaml` works today only through the alias `source`. Draft issue: `issue-drafts/action-must-do-something.md` (Eve decides when it is filed). `libs/transformer` change: Eve's O4 permission, tests first.

These three names actually mean "this deployment receives data pushed by an automated pipeline," which is a different thing — same shape of collision as the "Pipeline Issues" panel rename this session (F-26). Scoped as **Phase 2** (deployment-limitations testing), not mid-HOME-testing: touches 3 app features filenames, `rules_app_features_flags.md`'s full matrix, every launch command referencing them, deployment configs, docs, and `test_plan_2026-06.md` §2's CFG section. Needs a name decision first (e.g. `auto-static`/`auto-exploration-simple`/`auto-exploration-advanced`, or `fed-*`, or something naming the "data pushed automatically" property without using "pipeline") before any rename executes.

Recorded by the records agent, 2026-11-15, from the channel. Each line is one item: what the user sees first, then the decision with its date. A task code is named once, with its meaning. Tags are model and effort. The coordinator's blocks M114, M118 and M119 are context only.

Writing rule (Eve, channel M123, 2026-11-15): short plain sentences, one idea each. Say first what the user sees and what is wrong. Then the facts. Then the decision with its date. No task code or abbreviation without its plain meaning beside it.

**Category types and the Polars names**
- [ ] **CATEG-1, the shipped Categorical fields** `[sonnet/high]` — DECIDED 2026-11-15 (M119). What the user sees: a Categorical field orders its levels alphabetically on a plot, and that order can be wrong for a scale with a meaning. Decision: (1) nominal categories (no meaning in the order) become `String`; counts per category must stay the same. (2) Categories with a meaning in their order (for example low, medium, high; S, I, R, NI) become `Enum`, with the order written in the recipe. (3) `collection_date` and `submission_date` become `Date`, or are dropped if the shipped recipes do not use them (to check and to say which). (4) The wrong-type `value` in the stress recipe is corrected to numbers. (5) The figshare recipe keeps one Categorical field as the documented example. Order of work: first the data-scientist list (23 fields where the order changes, 5 `Enum` candidates; each one ORDINAL or NOMINAL with a one-line reason; pictures of the changed plots with counts per category before and after); then the migration, tests and a mutation check; then Eve sees the `Enum` list with its orders, and any plot whose counts differ. To confirm: the block says "the 57 and the nominal ones as String"; the number 57 is not explained. State: decided; the list is the first step.

The earlier note DEC27 (2026-11-13, "categorical" refused) was a choice about the shipped recipes, not a rule about the standard; the spec and ADR-100 text must say so. Tests for both types. State: the wording is written by the scribe.

- [ ] **SESSION-IMPORT-KEY-EXISTS-1** `[sonnet/medium]` — decided (D-0281, question 9 of the recipe-arguments report, option A, channel M298 of 57): when an imported session zip has a session key that already exists, the app asks the user in plain words (replace, keep both, or cancel) and never overwrites silently. Three parts: (1) the ask; (2) the member names of the zip are checked (no path outside the session folder), with a test that uses a crafted zip; (3) a size limit is PROPOSED to Eve (the number and the reason), not decided. The test must fail without the change. Size limit: read from the deployment file, default about three times a measured real session of the demo recipe; the numbers go to Eve first (D-0283). Added 2026-11-18 by the third review (branch `task/session-import`, head `e27d6228`; the review's findings are the items BUNDLE-IMPORT-LIMITS-1 and SESSION-SIZE-NUMBERS-1 of the 2026-11-18 findings, kept here by extension): (a) `app/modules/bundle_importer.py` has no member-name check and reads each member whole into memory, with no size limit; it writes only the base name of a member, so no path escape was found, but a very large zip would fill memory and the recipe id comes from a zip member name. (b) Numbers, WAITS FOR EVE: a real session is about 2 KB zipped and 5.5 KB unpacked; ten saves about 9.4 KB and 24 KB; "three times a real session" would refuse a session with about seven saves. PROVISIONAL default on the branch: 1 MB zipped, 10 MB unpacked (keys `session_import.max_zip_mb`, `session_import.max_unpacked_mb`). Evidence: `tmpAI/panel/session_import/REVIEW.md`.

- [x] **FILTER-FORM-RESET-1** `[sonnet/high]` — DONE 2026-11-19, merged `8f8474a9`. What a user saw: in HOME's Filters panel the chosen column jumped back to the first column after a change of "Data to show", and the value list did not follow the rows. Cause: the form read the table directly, so every recompute re-rendered it, and a render that began before the server had the click sent the old column back; the value list was checked by the text of the Polars plan, which Polars cuts short for long lists, so two different lists could look equal. Fix: a signal that changes only when the columns, the types or the row identity change (stored-table keys and the audited steps in full; `app/modules/t3_audit_engine.py`, `app/handlers/filter_and_audit_handlers.py`, `app/handlers/home_theater.py`). Sources: `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_report.md`, `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_rows_signature_report.md`, `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_REVIEW.md`.
  Known limits (`tmpAI/coordinator/filter_rows_signature_report.md` section 8): a table file edited outside the app under its old key is not seen; a plot without a target table rebuilds the form on every recompute; the Effect still computes the table with the Filters panel closed (cost not measured).
  Decided 2026-11-19 by the owner (channel M335 of 62, option B of three: keep, with a note and a check): the repair stays on dev and on the recipe-change branch, nothing is reverted (merge `8f8474a9` is not rewritten; the note is added here). It is a STOPGAP, not the tier rule: what the filter form shows per viewed tier is the audit redesign (FILTERS-TIER-VISION-FOR-REDESIGN). The hold of M302 is lifted for this one repair only; every other held item stays held. The check of the tier rule is FILTER-TIER-CHECK-1.
- [ ] **FILTER-FORM-LOST-PICK-1** `[for the owner]`: OPEN, PARKED by the owner's decision of 2026-11-19 (channel M336 of 62, point 4; ledger number not yet given): not asked now; it is listed in QUESTIONS-PARKED-UNTIL-REFACTOR-STEP (i). The known fault stays documented: a column picked right after a REAL change of columns, while the plots are drawn, is lost (15 of 15 in the builder's scripted probe). Evidence: `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_report.md` and `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_REVIEW.md`. Probe: 15 of 15 tries (switch plot tab, choose `year` 0 to 300 ms later). Reasoned cause: the server cannot see a click that waits behind its running work. Options as first recorded: A accept it and document it (the form is rebuilt after a plot change; choose the column after the plot has loaded); B make the app less busy on a plot change (a performance task; it narrows the window, it does not remove it); C a notice (the server would have to guess what was clicked); D a small client-side piece that holds the last pick across the rebuild (removes the loss; excluded by the present rule unless the owner allows it). The builder recommends A, with B as a task, and D only if the owner accepts a client-side piece (reasoned). Sources: `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_report.md` (point 1 and the choice), `tmpAI/coordinator/filter_column_reset_REVIEW.md` (point 7); channel M327 of 78 (coordinator). See also FILTER-FORM-COLUMN-SNAPS-BACK-1 (HELD, older entry of the same family); not merged or closed here: the coordinator asks the owner's session.

Her words, verbatim: "we need a way for static deployment to put several data files and where to be fetch - I suspect it will be a case where no user interact but underhood the app will need read and write - eg. case where same recipe is used to show data that is reactuallized every week and pushed in a directory - or the case of a pipeline there might still need a tmp location that the app can read/write and not the user - have the coordinator investigate those cases and see how it could be done without being associated to a specific user and it can be a configuration specification". Her three cases: (a) one recipe shows data refreshed every week and pushed into a directory; (b) a pipeline that needs a tmp location the app can read and write and the user cannot; (c) a static deployment with several data files and where each is fetched. Questions to answer (the review session's list, not her words): how this is stated in the deployment specification without any link to a user (an app-owned working place: the stored tables' place or another named place; how it differs from the user place; its access word); what the app writes there and never writes; what happens when it is not writable (choice 5 of the deployment study, still open); what "fetched" means (where fetched files land, how the app knows they changed, the hash that keeps a figure reproducible); the cases she did not name (several refreshed folders; a failed or partial refresh while the app runs; two copies of the app on one folder; a refresh that changes the columns). Part of the one deployment specification (D-0417, D-0418). Where it is written: per her rule D-0419 and D-0421 this study is written in `.claude/design/studies/deployment_no_user/` from its first day IF she has confirmed the subfolder naming by then; until then its files are under `tmpAI/panel/deployment_no_user/` and are listed for the move.
